Like if I have to sit for a safety meeting or... one of those certification classes. My foot is always bouncing. Or I'm clicking a pen... picking at my sleeves. I guess I shift around a lot. People usually ask me to stop.\n\nDoctor: Do you often act on impulse without thinking through the consequences?\n\nPatient: Um... I'm not really sure. I guess... maybe? I don't really think about it like that. I just kind of... do things. I haven't really noticed.\n\nDoctor: Do you often have trouble paying attention to details or making careless mistakes?\n\nPatient: Um... no, not really. I mean, when I'm actually at my station, I do the work fine. I don't really mess up the parts or anything. I just... I don't know. I haven't really noticed any problems with that.\n\nDoctor: Do you often talk more than others in social situations?\n\nPatient: Um... yeah, I guess. I mean, I talk a lot at work. Like... during shift handoffs. I'll just keep explaining things or... making jokes, I guess. Even when I can tell the next person just wants to start working. I just... kind of keep going.\n\nDoctor: Do you often have trouble completing tasks or projects?\n\nPatient: Um... no, not really. I mean, my job is pretty repetitive, so... you just finish one thing and start the next. I don't really have big projects to do. So... I guess I get it done fine.\n\nDoctor: Were you more active or restless than your peers when you were a child?\n\nPatient: Um... yeah, I guess so. Like, even back in elementary school... I couldn't really stay in my seat. I got a lot of notes sent home. Just for... blurting stuff out, or talking over the teacher. Or just wandering around the room, I guess. So... yeah.\n\nDoctor: Do you often have strong mood swings?\n\nPatient: Um... no. Not really. I mean... my mood is just... fine, I guess. I don't really notice anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel restless or fidgety when you're at home or doing things you enjoy?\n\nPatient: Um... yeah. I guess. Like... at work, if I have to just stand still by the machine, or... if we have to sit through some training thing... I don't know. It just feels like... like this pressure building up inside me. And I just... I have to move. Like, I have to pace around, or stretch, or just... go do something else right then. So... yeah, I guess I do.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"
